\section{How to Use This Companion}\label{how-to-use-this-companion}

This companion is the project-wide audit surface. Use it when you want
to inspect the full corpus behind the standalone papers and the omnibus
dossier without reading repo internals.

\subsection{Navigation Guide}\label{navigation-guide}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  If a paper footnote sends you to an external report, article, court
  order, filing, or technical paper, open that live link first.
\item
  If a paper then points you to \texttt{Appendix\ A}, \texttt{B},
  \texttt{C}, \texttt{D}, or \texttt{E}, stay inside that paper to see
  why the source, claim, event, entity, or evidence boundary matters in
  that chapter.
\item
  If a paper mentions a corpus label such as \texttt{Source\ S-066},
  \texttt{Claim\ C-212}, \texttt{Event\ T-014}, or \texttt{Note\ N-097},
  use this companion to inspect the project-wide audit record for that
  item.
\item
  \texttt{Source} entries identify the real underlying document and
  where it appears across the dossier.
\item
  \texttt{Claim} entries tell you exactly what proposition the project
  extracted from the record and how it is labeled evidentially.
\item
  \texttt{Event} entries reconstruct dated turning points used across
  chapters.
\item
  \texttt{Note} entries summarize what a source proves, what it
  suggests, and where caution is required.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Evidence Labels}\label{evidence-labels}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{Documented\ Fact}: a claim the project treats as directly
  supported by the documentary record at the required threshold.
\item
  \texttt{Disputed\ Fact}: a claim with serious documented disagreement
  or unresolved institutional conflict.
\item
  \texttt{Hypothesis\ /\ Interpretation}: a bounded political or
  analytical reading built from cited facts, not a settled fact in
  itself.
\item
  \texttt{Speculative\ Narrative\ Risk}: a claim shape the dossier
  explicitly warns against because it outruns the record.
\end{itemize}
